### Adversarial Suffix Filtering: a Defense Pipeline for LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09602v1)
- **Authors**: David Khachaturov, Robert Mullins
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly embedded in autonomous systems and public-facing environments, yet they remain susceptible to jailbreak vulnerabilities that may undermine their security and trustworthiness. Adversarial suffixes are considered to be the current state-of-the-art jailbreak, consistently outperforming simpler methods and frequently succeeding even in black-box settings. Existing defenses rely on access to the internal architecture of models limiting diverse deployment, increase memory and computation footprints dramatically, or can be bypassed with simple prompt engineering methods. We introduce $\textbf{Adversarial Suffix Filtering}$ (ASF), a lightweight novel model-agnostic defensive pipeline designed to protect LLMs against adversarial suffix attacks. ASF functions as an input preprocessor and sanitizer that detects and filters adversarially crafted suffixes in prompts, effectively neutralizing malicious injections. We demonstrate that ASF provides comprehensive defense capabilities across both black-box and white-box attack settings, reducing the attack efficacy of state-of-the-art adversarial suffix generation methods to below 4%, while only minimally affecting the target model's capabilities in non-adversarial scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Adversarial Suffix Filtering (ASF), a novel defense pipeline designed to protect Large Language Models (LLMs) against adversarial suffix attacks.  These attacks involve appending malicious token sequences to prompts in order to subvert the LLM's intended behavior. ASF functions as an input preprocessor, detecting and filtering these suffixes before they can influence the LLM. The pipeline consists of four main stages: segmentation, segment classification (using a fine-tuned BERT model), post-processing (smoothing and keyword filtering), and filtering. The authors demonstrate that ASF significantly reduces the success rate of state-of-the-art suffix attacks across both black-box and white-box settings, with minimal impact on normal LLM performance. They highlight the pipeline's model-agnostic nature and low computational overhead as key advantages for real-world deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper introduces a novel pipeline architecture for defending against adversarial suffix attacks on LLMs. While individual components like segmentation and BERT-based classification are not new, the combination of these elements into a lightweight, model-agnostic defense is a valuable contribution. Specifically, its ability to mitigate jailbreaking without heavy reliance on model-specific fine-tuning is a differentiating factor. The use of Segment Any Text for preprocessing text adds a unique element, since prior similar work has not used a model agnostic approach.

*   **Significance:**  The problem of prompt injection and jailbreaking attacks on LLMs is a critical concern for the safe deployment of these models. Adversarial suffixes pose a particularly challenging threat, so any defense strategy that effectively addresses this issue is significant. ASF's effectiveness across different attack settings (black-box and white-box) and its minimal impact on model utility enhances its practical value. The results that demonstrate the reduced ASR on benchmarks with common attacks is signficant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Model Agnosticism:**  ASF's ability to function as a front-end filter without requiring modifications to the underlying LLM is a major strength. This allows it to be easily integrated with a wide range of LLMs, including closed-source APIs.
    *   **Low Overhead:**  The pipeline's relatively low computational overhead makes it suitable for resource-constrained environments and latency-sensitive applications. It is also noted that adding ASF to the system does not increase token requirement or inference time.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The authors provide comprehensive experimental results demonstrating ASF's effectiveness against state-of-the-art adversarial suffix attacks and its preservation of model utility in non-adversarial settings.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Segmentation Dependency:** The pipeline's reliance on accurate segmentation is a potential weakness. As the authors acknowledge, imperfect segmentation can lead to either insufficient filtering (if the entire suffix is not identified) or over-filtering (if legitimate parts of the prompt are misclassified).
    *   **Suffix-Specific Design:** ASF is explicitly designed to address suffix-style attacks. It might not be effective against other types of prompt injection attacks that do not involve discernible suffixes, limiting its overall scope.
    *   **Hand-Tuned Heuristics:** The use of hand-tuned rules in the post-processing stage introduces a degree of brittleness and may require careful calibration for different deployment contexts.
    *   **Limited Error Reporting:** It would have been more convincing to see explicit error bars to indicate statistically significant results.

*   **Potential Influence:** ASF has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and effective defense mechanism against adversarial suffix attacks. Its model-agnostic nature and low overhead make it a viable option for real-world deployment, potentially contributing to the safer adoption of LLMs in various applications. Future research could focus on improving segmentation accuracy, extending the pipeline to handle other prompt injection techniques, and developing more robust and adaptive filtering rules.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**
    The paper presents a well-executed solution for a specific and highly relevant security problem in LLMs. Its strengths lie in its practical approach, its model-agnostic design, and its empirically demonstrated effectiveness.  While it has limitations, the identified weaknesses are clearly acknowledged by the authors and provide avenues for future research. The work does not represent a fundamental paradigm shift, but its practical value and strong evaluation justify a solid score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LightLab: Controlling Light Sources in Images with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09608v1)
- **Authors**: Nadav Magar, Amir Hertz, Eric Tabellion, Yael Pritch, Alex Rav-Acha, Ariel Shamir, Yedid Hoshen
- **Abstract**: We present a simple, yet effective diffusion-based method for fine-grained, parametric control over light sources in an image. Existing relighting methods either rely on multiple input views to perform inverse rendering at inference time, or fail to provide explicit control over light changes. Our method fine-tunes a diffusion model on a small set of real raw photograph pairs, supplemented by synthetically rendered images at scale, to elicit its photorealistic prior for relighting. We leverage the linearity of light to synthesize image pairs depicting controlled light changes of either a target light source or ambient illumination. Using this data and an appropriate fine-tuning scheme, we train a model for precise illumination changes with explicit control over light intensity and color. Lastly, we show how our method can achieve compelling light editing results, and outperforms existing methods based on user preference.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LightLab: Controlling Light Sources in Images with Diffusion Models" introduces a method for fine-grained, parametric control over light sources in single images using diffusion models.  The method leverages a combination of real raw photograph pairs and synthetically rendered images for training.  It addresses the challenge of physically plausible relighting by fine-tuning a diffusion model to control light intensity, color, and ambient illumination. The key is a data-driven approach, using paired images (light on/off) and exploiting the linearity of light. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of this approach on various images, showcasing applications like light intensity control, color adjustments, consecutive edits, and applying illumination changes to non-photorealistic images. A user study confirms that the proposed method outperforms existing relighting techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its approach to parametrically controlling light sources *directly* within a diffusion model framework using a blend of real and synthetic data. While diffusion models have been applied to relighting tasks, providing explicit, user-controlled parameters for light source intensity, color, and ambient illumination with this level of realism is a relatively new direction. Existing methods often rely on indirect control (e.g., text prompts, scribbles) or require multiple input views. The emphasis on physically plausible relighting by incorporating the linearity of light and a well-considered tone-mapping strategy further enhances the novelty. The synthesis of a mixed dataset using captured and synthetic images is also a valuable contribution towards bridging the realism gap.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the practical implications of enabling intuitive and precise light editing in single images. The ability to manipulate light sources after image capture has many applications, from photo enhancement and artistic expression to correcting lighting imperfections. The user study provides evidence that users prefer the light edits of this method over existing techniques. The paper provides a solid base on which future relighting tools can be built.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Data-driven Approach:** The paper's strength lies in its data-driven approach, carefully curating a dataset that combines the realism of real photographs with the control and scalability of synthetic rendering.
    *   **Explicit Control:**  The method provides explicit parametric control, allowing users to adjust light intensity, color, and ambient illumination directly.
    *   **Physical Plausibility:**  The incorporation of the linearity of light and careful tone-mapping contributes to physically plausible relighting results.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper includes a thorough evaluation with quantitative metrics, qualitative comparisons, and a user study, demonstrating the method's effectiveness and user preference.
    *   **Code/Data Availability:** The code and data will promote follow-on works and increases reproducability and usage.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Diversity:** The method is mainly demonstrated on indoor scenes with limited diversity in light sources and geometries. The reliance on a specific type of "visible" light source may limit its generalizability to more complex lighting scenarios or non-traditional light sources (e.g., indirect bounce lighting).
    *   **Bias in Dataset:** The results show dataset bias towards lighting effects and specific light sources due to fine-tuning a diffusion model and not training it from scratch.
    *   **Calibration Errors**: Inherent limitations to calibration errors in real photographs that introduce error measurements.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in image editing and relighting, particularly within the domain of generative models. The explicit light source control and emphasis on physical plausibility are valuable directions.  The combined real/synthetic data training strategy could be adopted in other image manipulation tasks.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**
The paper offers a notable contribution to the field of image relighting using diffusion models. The key innovation is providing explicit parametric control over light source properties (intensity, color, and ambient light), while emphasizing physically plausible results achieved through a combination of real and synthetic training data. The evaluation, including a user study, strengthens the claims and demonstrates the practical value of the approach. While the method demonstrates strong performance, there are limitations regarding scene diversity and potential biases introduced by the training dataset which prevents it from receiving an 8 or above. Nevertheless, LightLab presents a valuable advance and provides a strong foundation for future research in this area. The high impact and solid foundation merit a good score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Customizing a Large Language Model for VHDL Design of High-Performance Microprocessors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09610v1)
- **Authors**: Nicolas Dupuis, Ravi Nair, Shyam Ramji, Sean McClintock, Nishant Chauhan, Priyanka Nagpal, Bart Blaner, Ken Valk, Leon Stok, Ruchir Puri
- **Abstract**: The use of Large Language Models (LLMs) in hardware design has taken off in recent years, principally through its incorporation in tools that increase chip designer productivity. There has been considerable discussion about the use of LLMs in RTL specifications of chip designs, for which the two most popular languages are Verilog and VHDL. LLMs and their use in Verilog design has received significant attention due to the higher popularity of the language, but little attention so far has been given to VHDL despite its continued popularity in the industry. There has also been little discussion about the unique needs of organizations that engage in high-performance processor design, and techniques to deploy AI solutions in these settings. In this paper, we describe our journey in developing a Large Language Model (LLM) specifically for the purpose of explaining VHDL code, a task that has particular importance in an organization with decades of experience and assets in high-performance processor design. We show how we developed test sets specific to our needs and used them for evaluating models as we performed extended pretraining (EPT) of a base LLM. Expert evaluation of the code explanations produced by the EPT model increased to 69% compared to a base model rating of 43%. We further show how we developed an LLM-as-a-judge to gauge models similar to expert evaluators. This led us to deriving and evaluating a host of new models, including an instruction-tuned version of the EPT model with an expected expert evaluator rating of 71%. Our experiments also indicate that with the potential use of newer base models, this rating can be pushed to 85% and beyond. We conclude with a discussion on further improving the quality of hardware design LLMs using exciting new developments in the Generative AI world.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the customization of Large Language Models (LLMs) for VHDL code understanding and explanation within the context of high-performance microprocessor design. Addressing the underrepresentation of VHDL in LLM research compared to Verilog, the authors detail their approach to building a VHDL-specific LLM, emphasizing the unique challenges of handling proprietary design data securely. The work covers data acquisition and preprocessing, test set generation tailored for code explanation and knowledge acquisition, extended pretraining (EPT) of a base LLM, instruction tuning (IT), and model merging techniques. They also explore the concept of using an LLM-as-a-judge for model evaluation and incorporate user feedback into the model refinement process. The results show improvements in expert evaluation scores for code explanations from a base model to EPT models and further gains through instruction tuning and model merging. The paper concludes with a discussion of future improvements and the potential for LLMs to enhance design cycles in the high-performance processor domain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its focus on VHDL, a language less explored than Verilog in the context of LLMs. The techniques themselves (extended pretraining, instruction tuning, model merging) are not novel individually, but their application to VHDL, specifically within a secure, industrial, high-performance processor design environment, adds a layer of practical contribution. The use of LLM-as-a-Judge for scalable evaluation has some novelty, especially in the context of limited SME availability.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work is moderate.
    *   **Positive aspects:** Addressing VHDL specifically is beneficial given its continued relevance in industries like aerospace and automotive. The emphasis on secure deployment within a corporate environment is practical and highlights the real-world challenges of using LLMs with proprietary data. The investigation of the LLM-as-a-judge approach is an interesting step towards automating model evaluation in scenarios where human expert time is limited.
    *   **Limitations:**  The paper's technical depth is somewhat limited. While the experiments are well described, the results are not exceptionally groundbreaking.  The performance improvements are incremental and the impact on the high-performance processor design process, while promising, is not quantified or demonstrated through concrete case studies in the paper. The reliance on a proprietary base LLM somewhat limits the generalizability of the findings to other researchers. More detailed discussion of VHDL-specific nuances and how they informed the EPT strategy would add value.

*   **Strengths:** The paper is well-structured and clearly written. The description of the secure infrastructure and data preprocessing pipeline is valuable for others working on similar projects. The exploration of different techniques (EPT, IT, model merging) provides a comprehensive overview of the LLM customization process. The focus on practical concerns such as data security and SME availability is commendable.

*   **Weaknesses:** The primary weakness is the incremental nature of the technical contribution. While improvements are shown, they are not drastic, and the LLM's capabilities are still far from fully automated design tasks. The lack of quantitative data on the reduction of design cycles or improvements in design quality is a missed opportunity. The paper lacks detailed ablation studies showing the impact of each components of pretraining data, and the impact of parameters. It does not explain how the best judge was selected for inference time. The comparison to base model seems not that fair because the instruction tuned data set for the base model is probably different.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence researchers to explore VHDL-specific LLMs and focus on practical considerations for industrial deployment. The LLM-as-a-judge approach might inspire others to develop automated evaluation techniques. However, the limited novelty and significance mean that its overall impact will likely be moderate.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

The score reflects a paper that makes a worthwhile, though incremental, contribution. The focus on VHDL, security, and the LLM-as-a-judge concept are positives. However, the relatively modest performance improvements, lack of quantitative impact on design cycles, and reliance on a proprietary LLM limit the paper's overall significance. It is a competent and well-written paper that addresses a relevant problem, but it doesn't represent a major breakthrough in the field. The work is more of an engineering effort in applying existing methods to a specific domain than a significant theoretical advance. A score of 6 acknowledges the practical value of the work while recognizing its limitations in terms of novelty and fundamental impact.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Tales of the 2025 Los Angeles Fire: Hotwash for Public Health Concerns in Reddit via LLM-Enhanced Topic Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09665v1)
- **Authors**: Sulong Zhou, Qunying Huang, Shaoheng Zhou, Yun Hang, Xinyue Ye, Aodong Mei, Kathryn Phung, Yuning Ye, Uma Govindswamy, Zehan Li
- **Abstract**: Wildfires have become increasingly frequent, irregular, and severe in recent years. Understanding how affected populations perceive and respond during wildfire crises is critical for timely and empathetic disaster response. Social media platforms offer a crowd-sourced channel to capture evolving public discourse, providing hyperlocal information and insight into public sentiment. This study analyzes Reddit discourse during the 2025 Los Angeles wildfires, spanning from the onset of the disaster to full containment. We collect 385 posts and 114,879 comments related to the Palisades and Eaton fires. We adopt topic modeling methods to identify the latent topics, enhanced by large language models (LLMs) and human-in-the-loop (HITL) refinement. Furthermore, we develop a hierarchical framework to categorize latent topics, consisting of two main categories, Situational Awareness (SA) and Crisis Narratives (CN). The volume of SA category closely aligns with real-world fire progressions, peaking within the first 2-5 days as the fires reach the maximum extent. The most frequent co-occurring category set of public health and safety, loss and damage, and emergency resources expands on a wide range of health-related latent topics, including environmental health, occupational health, and one health. Grief signals and mental health risks consistently accounted for 60 percentage and 40 percentage of CN instances, respectively, with the highest total volume occurring at night. This study contributes the first annotated social media dataset on the 2025 LA fires, and introduces a scalable multi-layer framework that leverages topic modeling for crisis discourse analysis. By identifying persistent public health concerns, our results can inform more empathetic and adaptive strategies for disaster response, public health communication, and future research in comparable climate-related disaster events.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Tales of the 2025 Los Angeles Fire: Hotwash for Public Health Concerns in Reddit via LLM-Enhanced Topic Modeling" presents a study analyzing Reddit discourse during a hypothetical 2025 wildfire event in Los Angeles. The authors collected a dataset of Reddit posts and comments related to the Palisades and Eaton fires and applied a hierarchical topic modeling framework that combines Latent Dirichlet Allocation (LDA) and BERTopic, enhanced by Large Language Models (LLMs) and human-in-the-loop (HITL) refinement. The framework categorizes topics into Situational Awareness (SA) and Crisis Narratives (CN).  The study aims to understand public perceptions and communication of public health risks in real-time, identify persistent public health concerns, and inform disaster response strategies.  Key findings include the alignment of SA categories with real-world fire progression, identification of co-occurring categories related to public health and safety, and analysis of grief and mental health risks in CN instances. The authors also released the annotated dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies primarily in its specific application and integration of existing techniques. Applying topic modeling to Reddit data during a hypothetical wildfire scenario is not entirely novel, but the integration of LLMs and HITL refinement to enhance topic modeling, the development of a hierarchical SA/CN framework *and* releasing the annotated dataset adds some new aspects. Using both LDA and BERTopic and a multi-layered classification schema for topic analysis is also a strength.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is moderate. The findings on public health concerns and emotional responses during the hypothetical wildfire could be useful for informing disaster response and public health communication strategies *if* the 2025 data is consistent with patterns identified in analysis of actual events. The annotation of the dataset will enable the development of NLP models in the future. The approach itself presents a potentially scalable method for analyzing crisis discourse.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The use of a hierarchical topic modeling framework provides a structured approach to analyzing complex social media data.
    *   The integration of LLMs and human-in-the-loop refinement enhances the interpretability and accuracy of the topic modeling results.
    *   The analysis of both SA and CN categories provides a comprehensive understanding of public discourse during the hypothetical wildfire event.
    *   The release of the annotated dataset is a valuable contribution to the research community.
    *   The consideration of geographic variation in public health concerns.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The analysis is based on a *hypothetical* event, which limits the generalizability and practical applicability of the findings. This is a significant drawback, as real-world events often unfold in unpredictable ways, and public responses can vary depending on the specific context. It's unclear how closely the dataset was constructed to resemble a real world event.
    *   The paper focuses primarily on the methodological aspects of the study, with less emphasis on the actionable insights that can be derived from the findings.
    *   The analysis relies on data from Reddit, which may not be representative of the entire population affected by wildfires.
    *   Limited discussion of bias mitigation strategies in both data collection and model training.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research on disaster response and public health communication by providing a scalable and adaptable framework for analyzing social media discourse. The release of the annotated dataset could also stimulate further research in this area. However, its *hypothetical* nature significantly limits its impact compared to studies analyzing real events.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper's primary weakness lies in its use of a hypothetical scenario. While the methodological approach is sound and the integration of various techniques is commendable, the fact that the data is not based on a real event significantly diminishes its practical relevance and potential impact. The findings might not accurately reflect the complexities and nuances of public discourse during an actual wildfire, making it difficult to generalize the results or apply them directly to real-world disaster response strategies. The strengths in methodology and data sharing push the evaluation up, but the use of a hypothetical dataset significantly impacts the utility and importance of the study.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SI
- **Score**: 6/10

### System Prompt Optimization with Meta-Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09666v1)
- **Authors**: Yumin Choi, Jinheon Baek, Sung Ju Hwang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown remarkable capabilities, with optimizing their input prompts playing a pivotal role in maximizing their performance. However, while LLM prompts consist of both the task-agnostic system prompts and task-specific user prompts, existing work on prompt optimization has focused on user prompts specific to individual queries or tasks, and largely overlooked the system prompt that is, once optimized, applicable across different tasks and domains. Motivated by this, we introduce the novel problem of bilevel system prompt optimization, whose objective is to design system prompts that are robust to diverse user prompts and transferable to unseen tasks. To tackle this problem, we then propose a meta-learning framework, which meta-learns the system prompt by optimizing it over various user prompts across multiple datasets, while simultaneously updating the user prompts in an iterative manner to ensure synergy between them. We conduct experiments on 14 unseen datasets spanning 5 different domains, on which we show that our approach produces system prompts that generalize effectively to diverse user prompts. Also, our findings reveal that the optimized system prompt enables rapid adaptation even to unseen tasks, requiring fewer optimization steps for test-time user prompts while achieving improved performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "System Prompt Optimization with Meta-Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the overlooked problem of optimizing *system* prompts for large language models (LLMs).  Unlike most existing work that focuses on optimizing user prompts for specific tasks, this paper aims to learn system prompts that are task-agnostic and generalizable across diverse user prompts and unseen tasks.  The authors formulate this as a bilevel optimization problem: a higher-level objective of optimizing the system prompt for generalization, coupled with a lower-level objective of optimizing user prompts for task-specific performance.  They propose a meta-learning framework called MetaSPO to tackle this problem. MetaSPO meta-trains the system prompt over multiple source tasks, simultaneously updating user prompts iteratively to ensure synergy. Experiments on 14 unseen datasets across 5 domains demonstrate MetaSPO's effectiveness in both unseen generalization and test-time adaptation scenarios, outperforming baselines and showing improved efficiency in user prompt optimization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in identifying and formally defining the bilevel system prompt optimization problem.  While there's been a lot of work on prompt engineering, the specific focus on learning generalizable *system* prompts and the bilevel formulation are a distinct contribution.  The application of meta-learning to this problem is also novel, although meta-learning itself is a well-established technique.  The idea of iterative system and user prompt co-optimization to achieve synergy is another valuable aspect.

* **Significance:** Optimizing system prompts has the potential to significantly improve the efficiency and applicability of LLMs. By learning generalizable system prompts, we can reduce the need for task-specific prompt engineering, making LLMs more accessible and usable across diverse applications. The paper's positive empirical results suggest that the proposed approach can lead to substantial performance gains and faster adaptation to new tasks.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Problem Formulation:** The clear articulation of the bilevel optimization problem is a major strength.  It provides a formal framework for tackling the challenges of learning generalizable system prompts.
    * **Meta-Learning Approach:** Using meta-learning is a suitable and well-justified choice for learning generalizable knowledge across diverse tasks. The design of the MetaSPO framework, with iterative system and user prompt optimization, is also well-thought-out.
    * **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation on a diverse set of tasks and domains.  The inclusion of both unseen generalization and test-time adaptation scenarios provides a strong assessment of the proposed method's capabilities. The results are statistically significant and clearly demonstrate the superiority of MetaSPO over baselines.
    * **Ablation Studies and Analysis:** The ablation studies and additional analyses (e.g., analysis of similarity between source and target tasks, cross-domain generalization, and the impact of attention scores) provide valuable insights into the behavior and effectiveness of MetaSPO. The qualitative results on the optimized system prompts (in Appendix C) also lend support to the approach.
    * **Robustness Analyses:** The paper has included robustness tests for MetaSPO over different numbers of source tasks, as well as by switching the optimisation LLM. This provides strength to the claim that MetaSPO is generalisable.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on Optimizer LLM:** The paper acknowledges the dependence of MetaSPO on the capability of the optimizer LLM as a limitation. Although they show MetaSPO works effectively even with a small open-source optimizer, its upper-bound performance remains constrained by the optimizer's capability. While this is a common limitation of prompt optimization techniques, it's important to acknowledge the practical implications, especially for resource-constrained settings.
    * **Computational Cost:** The computational costs are not insubstantial, depending heavily on how many calls are made to the smaller-than-baseline LLMs.
    * **Limited Exploration of Optimization Techniques within MetaSPO:** While the authors demonstrate the flexibility of MetaSPO by incorporating different prompt optimization strategies, they could have explored a wider range of techniques and investigated how different combinations impact performance.  A more detailed comparison of various inner and outer loop optimization techniques would be valuable.
    * **Lack of comparison against state-of-the-art user prompt optimization methods:** As claimed in the paper's introduction, this paper is designed for optimising system prompts; however, if used together with state-of-the-art user prompt optimization methods, the results will likely be higher.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in prompt engineering and LLM optimization.  It highlights the importance of system prompt optimization and provides a practical framework for tackling this problem. The findings could also inform the design of more efficient and generalizable LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **8** to this paper. The novelty of defining and addressing the bilevel system prompt optimization problem, coupled with the well-designed MetaSPO framework and comprehensive experimental evaluation, constitutes a significant contribution. The paper addresses an important problem in the field of LLMs, provides a practical solution with strong empirical results, and offers valuable insights into the behavior and effectiveness of the proposed approach. The weaknesses, while important to consider, do not detract significantly from the overall value of the paper. The paper sets a strong foundation for future research in this area and has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and generalizable LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 0/10

### EWMBench: Evaluating Scene, Motion, and Semantic Quality in Embodied World Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09694v1)
- **Authors**: Hu Yue, Siyuan Huang, Yue Liao, Shengcong Chen, Pengfei Zhou, Liliang Chen, Maoqing Yao, Guanghui Ren
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in creative AI have enabled the synthesis of high-fidelity images and videos conditioned on language instructions. Building on these developments, text-to-video diffusion models have evolved into embodied world models (EWMs) capable of generating physically plausible scenes from language commands, effectively bridging vision and action in embodied AI applications. This work addresses the critical challenge of evaluating EWMs beyond general perceptual metrics to ensure the generation of physically grounded and action-consistent behaviors. We propose the Embodied World Model Benchmark (EWMBench), a dedicated framework designed to evaluate EWMs based on three key aspects: visual scene consistency, motion correctness, and semantic alignment. Our approach leverages a meticulously curated dataset encompassing diverse scenes and motion patterns, alongside a comprehensive multi-dimensional evaluation toolkit, to assess and compare candidate models. The proposed benchmark not only identifies the limitations of existing video generation models in meeting the unique requirements of embodied tasks but also provides valuable insights to guide future advancements in the field. The dataset and evaluation tools are publicly available at https://github.com/AgibotTech/EWMBench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EWMBENCH, a benchmark designed to evaluate Embodied World Models (EWMs).  The benchmark aims to assess how well these models generate physically grounded and action-consistent videos given language instructions and initial visual scenes. EWMBENCH focuses on three key aspects: visual scene consistency, motion correctness, and semantic alignment.  The benchmark includes a curated dataset of robotic manipulation tasks and a suite of evaluation metrics including adaptations of existing metrics and new scene consistency metric. The authors evaluate several existing video generation models on the benchmark, demonstrating its ability to identify limitations in these models when applied to embodied tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's primary novelty lies in its focus on benchmarking EWMs, a relatively new and important area. While previous video generation benchmarks exist, they primarily target general video generation and often lack the specific metrics needed to assess the physical plausibility and action consistency required for embodied AI. The creation of a dedicated dataset and the development of adapted metrics such as the scene consistency metric based on a fine-tuned DINOv2 model, alongside metrics considering robot manipulation task decomposition, are significant contributions.  The application of LLMs for scoring and the punishment of logical errors is a notable idea.

**Significance:**

The development of robust EWM benchmarks is crucial for advancing research in areas like robotics, embodied AI, and simulation.  EWMBENCH offers a standardized way to evaluate and compare different EWMs, facilitating progress by providing a clear target for model development. The analysis of current models, revealing their strengths and weaknesses in the embodied context, is valuable for guiding future research directions.  The focus on robotic manipulation tasks within the EWM context is also relevant, because robotics has seen great use in world models. The open-sourcing of the dataset and evaluation tools increases the benchmark's impact by enabling other researchers to use and build upon it.

**Strengths:**

*   **Targeted Focus:** Directly addresses the evaluation gap for EWMs.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Considers multiple relevant dimensions (scene, motion, semantics).
*   **Dataset & Tooling:** Provides a curated dataset and open-source evaluation tools.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** Offers insights into the performance of existing models.
*   **LLM integration**: Integrates language models for high-level task understanding and evaluation.
*   **Robotics-centric Design:** Focuses on robotic manipulation tasks, a key area for world models.
*   **Scene Consistency Evaluation**: Leverages finetuned DINOv2 models to better isolate agent features.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Task Scope:** The evaluation is primarily focused on robotic manipulation tasks. Expanding the benchmark to include other embodied tasks (e.g., navigation, interaction with virtual environments) would broaden its applicability.
*   **Reliance on Existing Models:** While the scene consistency metric finetunes an existing model, there may be even further untapped novel metrics available.
*   **Action Conditional Evaluation:** Limited evaluation on only Image-Text-to-Video setting
*   **End-Effector Trajectory Limitation:** Method only focuses on trajectory of end effector and not total robotic arm
*   **Fixed viewpoint:** Current evaluation is conducted in fixed view-point scenes.

**Influence:**

EWMBENCH has the potential to significantly influence research in embodied AI and robotics by providing a much-needed benchmark for EWMs. It can drive model development, facilitate comparisons, and accelerate progress in these fields.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the paper's targeted focus, comprehensive evaluation, and valuable contributions to the field, I assign a score of 7. The paper addresses a crucial need by providing a specialized benchmark for evaluating EWMs, an area that has been relatively under-explored. However, some limitations in task scope and the reliance on existing video datasets prevent it from achieving a higher score. The open-sourcing of the benchmark solidifies its place in the research community, enabling a broader scope of investigation.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### VeriFact: Enhancing Long-Form Factuality Evaluation with Refined Fact Extraction and Reference Facts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09701v1)
- **Authors**: Xin Liu, Lechen Zhang, Sheza Munir, Yiyang Gu, Lu Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) excel at generating long-form responses, but evaluating their factuality remains challenging due to complex inter-sentence dependencies within the generated facts. Prior solutions predominantly follow a decompose-decontextualize-verify pipeline but often fail to capture essential context and miss key relational facts. In this paper, we introduce VeriFact, a factuality evaluation framework designed to enhance fact extraction by identifying and resolving incomplete and missing facts to support more accurate verification results. Moreover, we introduce FactRBench , a benchmark that evaluates both precision and recall in long-form model responses, whereas prior work primarily focuses on precision. FactRBench provides reference fact sets from advanced LLMs and human-written answers, enabling recall assessment. Empirical evaluations show that VeriFact significantly enhances fact completeness and preserves complex facts with critical relational information, resulting in more accurate factuality evaluation. Benchmarking various open- and close-weight LLMs on FactRBench indicate that larger models within same model family improve precision and recall, but high precision does not always correlate with high recall, underscoring the importance of comprehensive factuality assessment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VERIFACT: Enhancing Long-Form Factuality Evaluation with Refined Fact Extraction and Reference Facts":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of evaluating the factuality of long-form text generated by large language models (LLMs). It identifies two main shortcomings of existing methods: (1) failure to capture complex inter-sentence dependencies leading to incomplete fact extraction, and (2) an overemphasis on precision with insufficient attention to recall (i.e., coverage of relevant facts). To address these issues, the authors introduce VERIFACT, a factuality evaluation framework that enhances fact extraction by identifying and resolving incomplete and missing facts. Furthermore, they present FACTRBENCH, a benchmark designed to evaluate both precision and recall in long-form model responses, featuring reference fact sets derived from advanced LLMs and human-written answers. The empirical evaluations demonstrate that VERIFACT improves fact completeness and leads to more accurate factuality assessment. Benchmarking LLMs on FACTRBENCH reveals insights into the precision and recall trade-offs of different model architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a real problem:** Evaluating the factuality of LLM-generated long-form text is a significant and pressing problem. The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and provides a practical solution.
*   **Novelty in Fact Extraction:** The key contribution is the focus on resolving incomplete and missing facts. The approach of identifying causes of incompleteness (missing comparandum, omitted condition, etc.) and then using LLMs to refine these facts seems effective, although the implementation relies heavily on LLM prompting, which could introduce biases.
*   **Comprehensive Benchmark (FACTRBENCH):** Creating FACTRBENCH is a valuable contribution. The inclusion of both real-world queries (FactBench) and recent Reddit questions enhances its relevance.  More importantly, the inclusion of human-written/LLM-generated reference facts and corresponding webpages enables a recall-oriented analysis which is missing in the other benchmarks. Releasing associated webpages also ensures reproducibility.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides thorough experimental results, showing that VERIFACT reduces incomplete facts and that FACTRBENCH reveals interesting trade-offs in precision and recall among different LLMs.
* **Focus on Inter-Sentence Dependencies:** A significant strength is the direct attempt to address the challenge of inter-sentence dependencies in factuality evaluation, a complex area not fully explored in previous works. The framework's ability to capture causal relations, as highlighted in Figure 1, adds considerable value.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on LLMs:** The entire VERIFACT pipeline depends heavily on the ability of LLMs to accurately identify, classify, and refine facts. This dependence could be a source of error and bias. While an ensemble approach is used, inherent limitations of LLMs in reasoning may persist. The paper doesn't fully address how to ensure LLM explanations are themselves factual or unbiased.
*   **Prompt Engineering:** The effectiveness of VERIFACT hinges on the quality of the prompts used to guide the LLMs.  While the appendix provides examples, a more detailed analysis of the prompt design process and its impact on performance would be beneficial. This is important because the performance of this pipeline could fluctuate depending on prompt engineering tricks.
*   **Computational Cost:** The multiple LLM passes required by VERIFACT make it computationally expensive, which could limit its practicality. This weakness is acknowledged in the Limitations section, but it is a major consideration. The authors should provide some measure of computational cost (e.g., average inference time per response).
*   **Reference Fact Set Completeness:** The authors also acknowledge the reliance on completeness of reference fact sets, and even human and LLM-derived reference facts might not cover all relevant facets of a query.
* **Generalizability Across Datasets:** While the paper focuses on FactBench and Reddit prompts, the methodology's effectiveness on other types of long-form text (e.g., academic papers, news articles) remains unclear. A more robust demonstration of generalizability would increase the paper's impact.

**Significance and Potential Influence:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM factuality evaluation by directly addressing the problem of incomplete fact extraction and introducing a benchmark that prioritizes both precision and recall. It could potentially influence future research in this area by encouraging the development of more comprehensive evaluation metrics and more sophisticated fact extraction techniques. Additionally, FACTRBENCH offers a valuable resource for benchmarking LLMs on long-form factuality.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper addresses a significant problem with a novel approach and presents a valuable benchmark.  However, the heavy reliance on LLMs, the prompt engineering required, and the computational cost are significant limitations. The paper's impact on the field will depend on how these limitations are addressed in future work. The focus on inter-sentence relations and the introduction of FACTRBENCH are strong points that contribute to a high score. While the reliance on potentially biased LLM judges tempers the score, the framework's ability to capture causal relations and missing condition makes it significantly more complete than existing baselines. This contributes to it's strong impact in the factuality evaluation field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EnerVerse-AC: Envisioning Embodied Environments with Action Condition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09723v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxin Jiang, Shengcong Chen, Siyuan Huang, Liliang Chen, Pengfei Zhou, Yue Liao, Xindong He, Chiming Liu, Hongsheng Li, Maoqing Yao, Guanghui Ren
- **Abstract**: Robotic imitation learning has advanced from solving static tasks to addressing dynamic interaction scenarios, but testing and evaluation remain costly and challenging due to the need for real-time interaction with dynamic environments. We propose EnerVerse-AC (EVAC), an action-conditional world model that generates future visual observations based on an agent's predicted actions, enabling realistic and controllable robotic inference. Building on prior architectures, EVAC introduces a multi-level action-conditioning mechanism and ray map encoding for dynamic multi-view image generation while expanding training data with diverse failure trajectories to improve generalization. As both a data engine and evaluator, EVAC augments human-collected trajectories into diverse datasets and generates realistic, action-conditioned video observations for policy testing, eliminating the need for physical robots or complex simulations. This approach significantly reduces costs while maintaining high fidelity in robotic manipulation evaluation. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness of our method. Code, checkpoints, and datasets can be found at <https://annaj2178.github.io/EnerverseAC.github.io>.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the ENERVERSE-AC paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ENERVERSE-AC (EVAC), an action-conditional world model designed to generate future visual observations in robotic manipulation tasks. By conditioning on predicted agent actions, EVAC aims to provide a realistic and controllable environment for robotic inference, eliminating the need for physical robots or complex simulators during policy testing and evaluation. The architecture incorporates a multi-level action-conditioning mechanism, ray map encoding for multi-view image generation, and training data augmented with diverse failure trajectories for improved generalization. As both a data engine and evaluator, EVAC augments human-collected trajectories into diverse datasets and generates realistic, action-conditioned video observations for policy testing. Experimental results validate the effectiveness of the approach, showing high fidelity in robotic manipulation evaluation and reduced costs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper builds upon prior work, specifically EnerVerse, but introduces several novel components. The key innovations are the multi-level action-conditioning mechanism (spatial-aware pose injection and delta action attention module), ray map encoding for dynamic multi-view image generation (addressing the movement of wrist cameras), and the explicit inclusion of failure trajectories in the training data. While world models and action-conditioned video generation aren't entirely new concepts, EVAC's specific architecture and application to robotic manipulation with a focus on controllability and realistic evaluation represent a meaningful step forward.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical challenge in robotics imitation learning: the high cost and difficulty of testing and evaluating policies in real-world environments. By providing a controllable and realistic virtual environment, EVAC has the potential to significantly accelerate the development and deployment of robotic policies. The ability to generate diverse training data and evaluate policies without relying on physical robots is a valuable contribution. The paper's findings demonstrate a good correlation between EVAC evaluations and real-world performance, increasing confidence in the simulator's utility.

* **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Technically sound approach with detailed architectural components.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation, including ablation studies, comparisons to real-world data, and demonstration of policy evaluation and data augmentation capabilities.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   Addresses practical needs in robot imitation learning, with direct application to policy testing and data augmentation.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   The limitation regarding the representation of the end-effector by unit circle is not applicable to more complex dexterous hands. Adapting the framework to different robotic hardware configurations will require additional preprocessing steps and refinements.
    *   Multi-view inference is constrained by background noise, limiting the process to only 10 chunks.

* **Potential Influence:**  EVAC has the potential to influence several areas within robotics:
    *   **Imitation Learning:** Providing a cost-effective and scalable evaluation environment.
    *   **Reinforcement Learning:**  Facilitating the training of policies in a simulated environment before deployment on real robots.
    *   **Data Augmentation:** Improving policy robustness and generalization by generating diverse training datasets.
    *   **Robot Simulation:** Offering an alternative to traditional physical simulators with improved controllability and realism in certain aspects.

**Justification for the Score:**

While EVAC builds upon existing research, it introduces several meaningful innovations in action conditioning and addresses a practical need in robotic manipulation. The experimental results are compelling, and the paper has the potential to significantly impact the field by reducing the cost and complexity of policy evaluation and enabling more efficient data augmentation. The clear limitations and potential future directions identified in the paper also show a mature understanding of the technology and its limitations. For these reasons, the paper earns a high score, but not a perfect one due to the reliance on existing concepts and the limitations of the current implementation.
