### Rethinking Prompt Optimizers: From Prompt Merits to Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09930v1)
- **Authors**: Zixiao Zhu, Hanzhang Zhou, Zijian Feng, Tianjiao Li, Chua Jia Jim Deryl, Mak Lee Onn, Gee Wah Ng, Kezhi Mao
- **Abstract**: Prompt optimization (PO) offers a practical alternative to fine-tuning large language models (LLMs), enabling performance improvements without altering model weights. Existing methods typically rely on advanced, large-scale LLMs like GPT-4 to generate optimized prompts. However, due to limited downward compatibility, verbose, instruction-heavy prompts from advanced LLMs can overwhelm lightweight inference models and degrade response quality. In this work, we rethink prompt optimization through the lens of interpretable design. We first identify a set of model-agnostic prompt quality merits and empirically validate their effectiveness in enhancing prompt and response quality. We then introduce MePO, a merit-guided, lightweight, and locally deployable prompt optimizer trained on our preference dataset built from merit-aligned prompts generated by a lightweight LLM. Unlike prior work, MePO avoids online optimization reliance, reduces cost and privacy concerns, and, by learning clear, interpretable merits, generalizes effectively to both large-scale and lightweight inference models. Experiments demonstrate that MePO achieves better results across diverse tasks and model types, offering a scalable and robust solution for real-world deployment. Our model and dataset are available at: https://github.com/MidiyaZhu/MePO
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Rethinking Prompt Optimizers: From Prompt Merits to Optimization":

**Summary:**

The paper challenges the conventional prompt optimization (PO) paradigm that relies on large, advanced LLMs to generate optimized prompts. The authors argue that such prompts, often verbose and instruction-heavy, can overwhelm smaller inference models, degrading response quality. They propose a new approach, MePO (Merit-Guided Prompt Optimization), which focuses on interpretable prompt design merits. The paper identifies four key merits (Clarity, Precision, Concise Chain-of-Thought, and Preserve Original Information) and empirically validates their effectiveness. MePO uses a lightweight LLM trained on a preference dataset constructed from merit-aligned prompts, avoiding the need for expensive online optimization or reliance on large-scale models. Experiments demonstrate that MePO achieves better results across diverse tasks and model types, offering a scalable, privacy-preserving, and robust solution for real-world deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel perspective on prompt optimization by shifting the focus from solely relying on powerful LLMs to emphasizing interpretable prompt design principles. Identifying and formalizing prompt merits is a valuable contribution. Moreover, training a lightweight optimizer on these merits is a practical approach. The idea of downward compatibility when prompting different LLMs is also an important observation.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to democratize prompt optimization, making it accessible and effective even in resource-constrained environments. By avoiding the dependence on API-based models, MePO reduces costs, addresses privacy concerns, and enables local deployment. The improved downward and upward compatibility of MePO optimized prompts compared to prompts produced by API based LLMs is important and useful. The claim of effective learning from interpretable merits, which generalises across model sizes and families is also noteworthy.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides strong empirical evidence to support its claims, including evaluations across diverse tasks, model types, and datasets.
    *   **Interpretability:** The focus on interpretable prompt merits makes the approach transparent and allows for better understanding of effective prompt design.
    *   **Practicality:** MePO is a practical solution that addresses real-world constraints, such as limited resources and privacy concerns.
    *   **Detailed Analysis**: Detailed results are provided for each prompt optimization setting across a variety of datasets and models.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The identified prompt merits, while effective, may not be exhaustive. Further research is needed to explore other potential factors that contribute to prompt quality.
    *   **Dataset Dependency:** The performance of MePO depends on the quality and diversity of the training data. The process of creating the preference dataset is also reliant on DeepSeek-R1 to establish the merit for each prompt. The reliance of a different LLM and the inherent subjectivity of merit assignment may introduce unintended bias.
    *   **Lack of Exploration in Model Adaptation**: Model-specific adaptation of MePO is not explicitly explored. As pointed out in the paper, there is an advantage in using the same model for both optimization and inference, which provides more room for improvement.
    *   **Lack of Exploration of Interactive Feedback Integration**: The paper focuses on one-shot prompt optimization without feedback from end-users and inference models. Exploring integrating the feedback loop would further boost the performance of MePO.

*   **Potential Influence:** MePO has the potential to influence future research in prompt optimization by promoting a more interpretable and accessible approach. The concept of prompt merits could serve as a foundation for developing more effective prompt engineering techniques.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of prompt engineering. It offers a practical and effective solution that challenges the prevailing reliance on large, expensive models. While there are some limitations, the empirical evidence and interpretability of the approach justify a high score. The work provides a solid foundation for further research and development in this area.

**Score: 8.0**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### CartoAgent: a multimodal large language model-powered multi-agent cartographic framework for map style transfer and evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09936v1)
- **Authors**: Chenglong Wang, Yuhao Kang, Zhaoya Gong, Pengjun Zhao, Yu Feng, Wenjia Zhang, Ge Li
- **Abstract**: The rapid development of generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) presents new opportunities to advance the cartographic process. Previous studies have either overlooked the artistic aspects of maps or faced challenges in creating both accurate and informative maps. In this study, we propose CartoAgent, a novel multi-agent cartographic framework powered by multimodal large language models (MLLMs). This framework simulates three key stages in cartographic practice: preparation, map design, and evaluation. At each stage, different MLLMs act as agents with distinct roles to collaborate, discuss, and utilize tools for specific purposes. In particular, CartoAgent leverages MLLMs' visual aesthetic capability and world knowledge to generate maps that are both visually appealing and informative. By separating style from geographic data, it can focus on designing stylesheets without modifying the vector-based data, thereby ensuring geographic accuracy. We applied CartoAgent to a specific task centered on map restyling-namely, map style transfer and evaluation. The effectiveness of this framework was validated through extensive experiments and a human evaluation study. CartoAgent can be extended to support a variety of cartographic design decisions and inform future integrations of GenAI in cartography.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces CartoAgent, a novel multi-agent cartographic framework powered by multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for map style transfer and evaluation. The framework simulates the cartographic process through three key stages: preparation, map design, and evaluation. Different MLLMs act as agents with specific roles, collaborating to generate visually appealing and informative maps. CartoAgent separates style from geographic data, focusing on stylesheets without modifying the vector-based data, ensuring accuracy. The authors validate their framework through multi-scale and multi-source map style transfer experiments, human evaluation, and a detailed case study, demonstrating its effectiveness and potential for advancing cartography.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty*: The concept of leveraging MLLMs within a multi-agent framework for cartography is novel. It goes beyond simply using LLMs for text generation or basic spatial analysis.  The specific implementation, focusing on map style transfer and iterative refinement through agent interactions, represents a significant advancement. The system addresses a key limitation of existing AI-generated maps: the lack of control over aesthetic quality while maintaining geographic accuracy. The design also provides an avenue for future enhancement and automation.

*Significance*: CartoAgent holds considerable significance for the field of cartography. It simplifies and automates aspects of the map design process, making high-quality cartographic design more accessible to users without extensive expertise. By separating style and content, the framework tackles the critical issues of map accuracy and information conveyance, effectively using MLLM's visual capabilities and world knowledge.

*Strengths*:

*   **Well-defined Architecture:**  The multi-agent architecture with clear roles and interactions is a significant strength. It mirrors the workflow of a skilled cartographer, which aids in interpretability and allows for targeted improvements. The modular design also allows for easy expansion in capabilities.
*   **Emphasis on Accuracy:** The framework's focus on maintaining geographic accuracy by manipulating stylesheets rather than modifying core data is crucial, distinguishing it from many image-based generative approaches.
*   **Iterative Refinement:** The inclusion of a map reviewer agent and the iterative feedback loop significantly enhances the quality and aesthetic appeal of the final maps.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The comprehensive experimental results, including multi-scale and multi-source map style transfers and a human evaluation study, provides solid evidence of the framework's effectiveness and alignment with human preferences.
*   **Discussion of limitations and Ethical Issues**: The authors transparently discuss the limitations of their work, such as the challenge of balancing communicative clarity with aesthetic resemblance and the ethical implications of AI-generated maps, showing a high degree of scholarly awareness.

*Weaknesses*:

*   **Dependency on Pre-trained MLLMs:** The system's reliance on pre-trained MLLMs means its performance is directly tied to the capabilities and biases of these models. While GPT-4o is cutting edge, the framework's success hinges on future advancements in MLLMs. Also, there may be an associated increased financial cost to scale this technology.
*   **Limited Controllability over Icon Generation:**  The reliance on DALL-E for icon design, while effective, introduces a level of unpredictability and potential for errors (as noted in the paper with hallucinated text). More direct control over icon generation would improve the system.
*   **Manual Integration for Certain Elements:**  The need for manual integration of map elements like north arrows and scale bars using Mapbox GL JS is a drawback, suggesting room for improvement in fully automating the process.
*   **Computational Cost and Scalability**: While the paper doesn't explicitly address this, running multiple MLLM agents iteratively is likely computationally expensive. The scalability of the approach for large areas or complex map styles could be a concern.

*Potential Influence*: The framework could influence future research in cartography by providing a blueprint for integrating AI into map design. It promotes a focus on interpretable, controllable AI systems that respect cartographic principles and user preferences. The ideas of iterative design, multiple interacting agents, and separation of style from content are valuable contributions to the field.

**Overall**: CartoAgent demonstrates a well-conceived and carefully implemented system for automated map style transfer. Its novelty lies in its use of a multi-agent framework powered by MLLMs to produce high-quality maps while focusing on both aesthetic qualities and geographical accuracy, and its significance is demonstrated by experiments and a detailed user study. The main weaknesses are primarily tied to the inherent limitations and associated costs of the technology, which can continue to be updated and improved as the technology improves.

**Score: 8**

*Rationale*: CartoAgent represents a solid advancement in the application of AI to cartography, addressing key limitations of previous approaches and showing strong potential for future development. However, the dependencies on external MLLMs and manual integration needs keep it from achieving a truly exceptional rating.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 8/10

### Design and Evaluation of Generative Agent-based Platform for Human-Assistant Interaction Research: A Tale of 10 User Studies
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09938v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyi Xuan, Yiwen Wu, Xuhai Xu, Vinod Namboodiri, Mooi Choo Chuah, Yu Yang
- **Abstract**: Designing and evaluating personalized and proactive assistant agents remains challenging due to the time, cost, and ethical concerns associated with human-in-the-loop experimentation. Existing Human-Computer Interaction (HCI) methods often require extensive physical setup and human participation, which introduces privacy concerns and limits scalability. Simulated environments offer a partial solution but are typically constrained by rule-based scenarios and still depend heavily on human input to guide interactions and interpret results. Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have introduced the possibility of generative agents that can simulate realistic human behavior, reasoning, and social dynamics. However, their effectiveness in modeling human-assistant interactions remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we present a generative agent-based simulation platform designed to simulate human-assistant interactions. We identify ten prior studies on assistant agents that span different aspects of interaction design and replicate these studies using our simulation platform. Our results show that fully simulated experiments using generative agents can approximate key aspects of human-assistant interactions. Based on these simulations, we are able to replicate the core conclusions of the original studies. Our work provides a scalable and cost-effective approach for studying assistant agent design without requiring live human subjects. We will open source both the platform and collected results from the experiments on our website: https://dash-gidea.github.io/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GIDEA, a generative agent-based simulation platform designed to facilitate research on human-assistant interactions. Recognizing the challenges (time, cost, ethical considerations) of conducting human-in-the-loop experiments, GIDEA uses large language models (LLMs) to simulate human behavior, reasoning, and social dynamics. The authors replicate ten previously published studies on assistant agents, spanning aspects of interaction design, and demonstrate that GIDEA can approximate key aspects and core conclusions of these studies. The platform allows for scalable and cost-effective assistant agent design research without relying on live human subjects.  The paper details GIDEA's system design, consisting of interaction knowledge, context setup, and agent-avatar interaction modules. They outline how the platform handles configuration, scenario design, dynamic changes, and behavioral modeling, emphasizing traceability and reproducibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using generative agents for simulating human-assistant interactions has merit but isn't entirely novel. Other researchers have explored LLM-based simulation for human behavior. However, the *specific* application to human-assistant interaction research, with the platform, GIDEA, focusing on replicating existing studies, is a contribution. The platform integrates different research approaches, and incorporates human interactions at a high-fidelity, making it an insightful contribution.
*   **Significance:** The potential for GIDEA to reduce the time, cost, and ethical burdens associated with human-in-the-loop HCI experiments is highly significant. It provides a scalable and potentially generalizable alternative approach for researchers in this field. If GIDEA can accurately predict user behavior across various assistant agent designs, it could lead to more effective and user-centered technologies. The rigorous validation through replicating existing research also adds to the impact, making this a promising contribution. The modular nature allows for customization and adaptation, while the open-source nature promotes the field.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper accurately highlights the limitations of traditional HCI methods for studying assistant agents.
    *   **Comprehensive Platform Design:** GIDEA's architecture, with its knowledge, context, and interaction modules, provides a well-structured framework for simulation.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The replication-based approach, using ten diverse prior studies, provides a strong foundation for assessing GIDEA's effectiveness.  The use of both semantic similarity and interaction log analysis strengthens the evaluation.
    *   **Open Source Availability:** Releasing the platform and results ensures transparency and allows the community to build on this work.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLM Fidelity:** GIDEA's accuracy depends on the LLM's ability to realistically simulate human behavior. LLMs are known to exhibit biases, inconsistencies, and sometimes, illogical reasoning. If LLMs don't generate accurate results, there's an issue.
    *   **Simplified Behavioral Models:** While GIDEA incorporates personality traits, the behavioral models are still relatively simple. It is a simulation based on human knowledge and inputs, rather than actual human actions. Simulating emotional states will prove difficult. The LLM will never capture the entire complexity of real-world human behavior. The ten studies have proven to be a strong baseline, but testing and replication may not be enough.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The focus on replicating existing studies raises questions about GIDEA's ability to explore entirely *new* interaction paradigms or scenarios.
    *   **Potential for Overestimation:** Simulated avatars may engage differently than real human ones. While the simulation could estimate, it fails to entirely mirror the way actual human beings will engage in a setting.
    *   **Lack of Human-in-the-Loop Validation:** While GIDEA replicates existing results, it would be beneficial to validate GIDEA's predictive power directly with a small-scale human-in-the-loop study for a novel scenario.
*   **Impact:** The platform is useful, and is validated by testing with existing studies. It could be transformative in the sense it could be applied to numerous fields, and is flexible and adaptable for various domains in human-assistant interaction, not limited to smart homes, systems, voice assistants, or decision-support. The platform will be able to benefit those in human assistant interaction, and can allow for scalability of this industry.
*   **Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a valuable tool for advancing research in human-assistant interaction by mitigating the constraints of human-in-the-loop experimentation. GIDEA offers a robust framework, validated through rigorous replication of existing studies, that has the potential to increase scalability, reduce costs, and address ethical concerns. While GIDEA is not without limitations – primarily stemming from the reliance on LLM behavior and the simplification of human models – the authors are transparent about these challenges and the potential directions for further development. The significance of this contribution lies in creating a pathway towards more accessible and agile research practices in the human-computer interaction domain. The open-source availability greatly enhances its potential impact. The novelty, while not groundbreaking in its core concept, is well-executed within the specific problem domain, leading to this assigned score.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Personalizing Large Language Models using Retrieval Augmented Generation and Knowledge Graph
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09945v1)
- **Authors**: Deeksha Prahlad, Chanhee Lee, Dongha Kim, Hokeun Kim
- **Abstract**: The advent of large language models (LLMs) has allowed numerous applications, including the generation of queried responses, to be leveraged in chatbots and other conversational assistants. Being trained on a plethora of data, LLMs often undergo high levels of over-fitting, resulting in the generation of extra and incorrect data, thus causing hallucinations in output generation. One of the root causes of such problems is the lack of timely, factual, and personalized information fed to the LLM. In this paper, we propose an approach to address these problems by introducing retrieval augmented generation (RAG) using knowledge graphs (KGs) to assist the LLM in personalized response generation tailored to the users. KGs have the advantage of storing continuously updated factual information in a structured way. While our KGs can be used for a variety of frequently updated personal data, such as calendar, contact, and location data, we focus on calendar data in this paper. Our experimental results show that our approach works significantly better in understanding personal information and generating accurate responses compared to the baseline LLMs using personal data as text inputs, with a moderate reduction in response time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an approach to personalize large language models (LLMs) using Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) and Knowledge Graphs (KGs). The core idea is to use KGs, which store continuously updated personal data (specifically calendar information in this study), to provide context to the LLM during response generation. By using RAG to retrieve relevant information from the KG, the approach aims to mitigate hallucinations and generate more accurate and personalized responses.  The authors use smaller models to be able to deploy them on edge devices (smartphones). Experiments compare the proposed KG-augmented RAG approach with a baseline RAG approach (without KGs) using ROUGE and BLEU metrics.  The results suggest that the KG-based RAG approach improves understanding of personal information and response accuracy with comparable (or slightly reduced) response times.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of combining RAG with KGs for LLM personalization is not entirely novel. Prior work has explored similar ideas, especially within the context of information retrieval and question answering.  The novelty is present in several aspects:
    *   **Focus on On-Device Personalization:**  The paper emphasizes the use of smaller models specifically suitable for deployment on personal devices (smartphones).  This focus on practical, on-device use is a differentiator.
    *   **Specific Use Case (Calendar Data):**  The application to calendar data and smart replies is a specific instantiation of the general idea, with real-world implications. While the domain itself may not be deeply innovative, the practical application strengthens the paper.
    *   **Privacy Focus:** The explicit mention of the privacy advantage of keeping personal data on-device and not sending it to cloud-based LLM providers is a relevant and timely consideration.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its potential to contribute to more personalized and privacy-conscious LLM applications. The ability to generate accurate responses based on dynamically updated personal data on-device has clear benefits for user experience and data security. The focus on calendar data and smart replies could lead to improvements in assistive technologies.  The claim of reduced hallucinations is significant if it holds true across more diverse and complex datasets. The contribution is also significant as they have made their code and dataset publicly available.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of hallucinations and lack of personalization in LLMs.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The proposed KG-augmented RAG approach is well-defined and easy to understand.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The experimental evaluation provides evidence to support the effectiveness of the proposed approach, using standard metrics (ROUGE, BLEU) and comparing against a reasonable baseline.
    *   **Privacy Considerations:** The paper highlights the privacy benefits of on-device processing.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors make their code and dataset publicly available, enhancing the reproducibility of the work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Dataset:** The experiments are based on a synthetic calendar and conversation dataset generated using ChatGPT-4. While this is a reasonable starting point, the results may not generalize to real-world, more complex, and noisy data.
    *   **Lack of Comparisons to More Advanced Methods:** The comparison is against a standard RAG baseline. The evaluation could be strengthened by comparing to other state-of-the-art personalization techniques or more sophisticated methods for knowledge graph integration.
    *   **Evaluation Metric Limitations:** ROUGE and BLEU are not perfect measures of semantic accuracy or relevance, especially in the context of conversational responses.
    *   **Modest Gains:** While the results show improvements over the baseline, the magnitude of improvement is not overwhelmingly large. The reduction in execution time is small and could be subject to variations based on hardware and software configurations.
    *   **Knowledge Graph Construction:** The paper uses SpaCy for KG construction.  This is a basic approach and its performance is highly dependent on sentence structure and complexity.  A more robust and scalable KG extraction technique would enhance the method's real-world applicability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more personalized and privacy-preserving LLM applications. The emphasis on on-device processing and KG integration aligns with emerging trends in the field.  The open-source release of the code and dataset could encourage further research and development in this area.
Score: 6.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a reasonable, though not groundbreaking, approach to LLM personalization. The combination of RAG and KGs is a logical extension of existing research, and the focus on on-device deployment and privacy is a relevant contribution. However, the limitations of the dataset, the lack of comparisons with more advanced methods, and the modest performance gains prevent a higher score. The significance is somewhat diminished by the synthetic nature of the dataset and the use of a relatively simple KG construction technique.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Pre-Act: Multi-Step Planning and Reasoning Improves Acting in LLM Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09970v1)
- **Authors**: Mrinal Rawat, Ambuje Gupta, Rushil Goomer, Alessandro Di Bari, Neha Gupta, Roberto Pieraccini
- **Abstract**: The ReAct (Reasoning + Action) capability in large language models (LLMs) has become the foundation of modern agentic systems. Recent LLMs, such as DeepSeek-R1 and OpenAI o1/o3, exemplify this by emphasizing reasoning through the generation of ample intermediate tokens, which help build a strong premise before producing the final output tokens. In this paper, we introduce Pre-Act, a novel approach that enhances the agent's performance by creating a multi-step execution plan along with the detailed reasoning for the given user input. This plan incrementally incorporates previous steps and tool outputs, refining itself after each step execution until the final response is obtained. Our approach is applicable to both conversational and non-conversational agents. To measure the performance of task-oriented agents comprehensively, we propose a two-level evaluation framework: (1) turn level and (2) end-to-end. Our turn-level evaluation, averaged across five models, shows that our approach, Pre-Act, outperforms ReAct by 70% in Action Recall on the Almita dataset. While this approach is effective for larger models, smaller models crucial for practical applications, where latency and cost are key constraints, often struggle with complex reasoning tasks required for agentic systems. To address this limitation, we fine-tune relatively small models such as Llama 3.1 (8B & 70B) using the proposed Pre-Act approach. Our experiments show that the fine-tuned 70B model outperforms GPT-4, achieving a 69.5% improvement in action accuracy (turn-level) and a 28% improvement in goal completion rate (end-to-end) on the Almita (out-of-domain) dataset.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Pre-Act," an enhancement to the ReAct framework for LLM-based agents. Pre-Act improves agent performance by generating a multi-step execution plan with detailed reasoning for each action. This plan incorporates past actions and observations as context and is refined incrementally until the final response is obtained. The authors also propose a curriculum learning based fine-tuning strategy that allows smaller models (Llama 8B, 70B) to achieve performance comparable to or exceeding larger, proprietary LLMs. They also use a two-level evaluation framework with turn-level and end-to-end metrics to measure agent performance comprehensively. The experimental results show that Pre-Act outperforms ReAct and the fine-tuned smaller models achieve significantly better action accuracy and goal completion rates.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the idea of planning in LLM agents isn't entirely novel, the paper makes a strong contribution with *explicit* generation of a *detailed* multi-step plan with reasoning for *each* step.  This contrasts with ReAct's single-step reasoning approach. The curriculum learning approach for fine-tuning smaller models using datasets adapted for Pre-Act also adds value, particularly in making agents more practical for resource-constrained applications. The use of two-level evaluation framework (turn-level and end-to-end) makes the evaluation more robust. Overall, Pre-Act provides a more structured and interpretable approach to agentic reasoning. However, some of the methods have been previously used, so I am not sure if the method is really novel.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing practical limitations of LLM-based agents. The enhanced reasoning capabilities through multi-step planning improve the handling of complex tasks, and the fine-tuning strategy makes it feasible to deploy high-performing agents using smaller, more efficient models, leading to reduced latency and cost. The gains from this work are potentially substantial, opening up possibilities for more real-world applications of LLM agents. However, there are still things to improve. For example, since the fine-tuned models have a better performance, how can we make it better for pre-trained models?

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The Pre-Act approach is well-defined and addresses a specific weakness in current LLM agent frameworks.
    *   The experimental results are convincing, demonstrating substantial improvements over ReAct, especially with the fine-tuned models.
    *   The curriculum learning-based fine-tuning strategy is practical and effective.
    *   The comprehensive evaluation framework provides a more holistic view of agent performance.
    *   The inclusion of both conversational and non-conversational agent use cases demonstrates broad applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty, as noted above, could be argued since planning approaches exist and fine-tuning is well-established. The core novelty lies in integrating a planning step and combining it with an existing dataset, it may not be deemed significantly novel.
    *   The reliance on a proprietary dataset for fine-tuning might make it difficult for others to replicate the results precisely. The authors could consider releasing the dataset (or a subset) or exploring strategies to adapt public datasets for Pre-Act more effectively.
    *   The evaluation heavily relies on LLM-as-a-judge metrics, which are themselves subject to variability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:
    *   Promoting a more structured and planning-based approach to LLM agent design.
    *   Encouraging the use of smaller, more efficient models through effective fine-tuning strategies.
    *   Providing a more comprehensive evaluation framework for LLM agent performance.
    *   Highlighting the importance of combining reasoning and acting for complex task completion.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6.5**. The paper presents a clear and compelling approach to improving LLM agent performance through structured planning and targeted fine-tuning. The experimental results are strong and the two-level evaluation contributes to the value of the paper. Although some of the methods (e.g., planning and fine-tuning) have been previously used, the work does well to combine the method and provides an enhancement over previous LLM-based agents.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Analysing Safety Risks in LLMs Fine-Tuned with Pseudo-Malicious Cyber Security Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09974v1)
- **Authors**: Adel ElZemity, Budi Arief, Shujun Li
- **Abstract**: The integration of large language models (LLMs) into cyber security applications presents significant opportunities, such as enhancing threat analysis and malware detection, but can also introduce critical risks and safety concerns, including personal data leakage and automated generation of new malware. We present a systematic evaluation of safety risks in fine-tuned LLMs for cyber security applications. Using the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications framework, we assessed seven open-source LLMs: Phi 3 Mini 3.8B, Mistral 7B, Qwen 2.5 7B, Llama 3 8B, Llama 3.1 8B, Gemma 2 9B, and Llama 2 70B. Our evaluation shows that fine-tuning reduces safety resilience across all tested LLMs (e.g., the safety score of Llama 3.1 8B against prompt injection drops from 0.95 to 0.15). We propose and evaluate a safety alignment approach that carefully rewords instruction-response pairs to include explicit safety precautions and ethical considerations. This approach demonstrates that it is possible to maintain or even improve model safety while preserving technical utility, offering a practical path forward for developing safer fine-tuning methodologies. This work offers a systematic evaluation for safety risks in LLMs, enabling safer adoption of generative AI in sensitive domains, and contributing towards the development of secure, trustworthy, and ethically aligned LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the safety risks associated with fine-tuning large language models (LLMs) with pseudo-malicious cybersecurity data. It evaluates seven open-source LLMs using the OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications framework, demonstrating that fine-tuning reduces safety resilience across all tested models. The authors then propose and evaluate a safety alignment approach that rewords instruction-response pairs to incorporate explicit safety precautions and ethical considerations. This approach shows promise in maintaining or even improving model safety while preserving technical utility. The work provides a systematic evaluation of safety risks and a potential mitigation strategy, contributing to the development of secure and ethically aligned LLMs in cybersecurity applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper provides a systematic and well-structured evaluation of safety risks, using a recognized framework (OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications). This contributes to the field by providing a more organized approach to assessing LLM security.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The research addresses a very relevant problem: the security implications of using LLMs in cybersecurity, particularly the potential for fine-tuning to create models that can be misused.
*   **Novel Safety Alignment Approach:** The proposed safety alignment method, based on rewording instruction-response pairs, is a novel approach to mitigating safety risks. Its demonstration of maintaining or improving safety while preserving utility is a significant contribution.
*   **Reproducibility and Openness:** Using open-source models and providing detailed descriptions of the methods used contributes to the reproducibility of the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of Mitigation Evaluation:** The safety alignment approach is only evaluated on Llama 3 8B. While the results are promising, its generalizability to other models or different datasets is not fully established. A more thorough evaluation across all tested models, including different size classes, would be beneficial.
*   **Reliance on DeepSeek-R1 for transformation:** The data transformation relies on the LLM DeepSeek-R1 and manual inspection. Given that the CyberLLMInstruct dataset is large, a method utilizing LLMs to create a similar dataset in an automated fashion would have been beneficial.
*   **Limited Definition of Pseudo-Malicious Data:** While the description is generally understandable, the specific nuances of the "pseudo-malicious" dataset could be described in more detail. A more formal definition and justification for why the chosen data represents realistic threats would strengthen the argument. The current explanation could be interpreted as not truly simulating real-world attacks.
*   **OWASP Framework as a Measure:** It can be argued that OWASP Top 10 might not be the best framework to represent cybersecurity datasets, more specifically it is not designed to evaluate datasets used in LLM training.
*   **Scalability:** The evaluation is not particularly scalable. The manual nature of inspecting transformations makes it hard to scale the same process for larger cybersecurity datasets.
*   **Incomplete test suite:** Some probes (Supply Chain, System Prompt Leakage, and Unbounded Consumption) described in the section are not supported by the garak framework used in evaluation, indicating the potential for overlooked vulnerabilities.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper is moderately novel. While other works have explored LLM safety and fine-tuning risks, the systematic evaluation using the OWASP framework and the specific safety alignment approach focused on rewording instruction-response pairs contribute new insights. The practical implications for cybersecurity applications make this research significant.

**Potential Influence:**

This research could influence the development of safer fine-tuning methodologies for LLMs in sensitive domains. It may also inform security guidelines for using LLMs in cybersecurity applications.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a systematic evaluation of LLM safety risks related to fine-tuning, highlighting a relevant problem in the use of LLMs for cybersecurity. The presented safety alignment method offers a practical and promising mitigation strategy. However, the scope of the evaluation of the safety alignment approach is somewhat limited, and there is some lack of clarity regarding the pseudo-malicious dataset. These limitations, as well as the limited support for select OWASP probes within the garak framework limit a higher score. Therefore, the score reflects a solid and valuable contribution with clear practical relevance, but also opportunities for further research to strengthen the findings and address the identified weaknesses.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Ordered-subsets Multi-diffusion Model for Sparse-view CT Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09985v1)
- **Authors**: Pengfei Yu, Bin Huang, Minghui Zhang, Weiwen Wu, Shaoyu Wang, Qiegen Liu
- **Abstract**: Score-based diffusion models have shown significant promise in the field of sparse-view CT reconstruction. However, the projection dataset is large and riddled with redundancy. Consequently, applying the diffusion model to unprocessed data results in lower learning effectiveness and higher learning difficulty, frequently leading to reconstructed images that lack fine details. To address these issues, we propose the ordered-subsets multi-diffusion model (OSMM) for sparse-view CT reconstruction. The OSMM innovatively divides the CT projection data into equal subsets and employs multi-subsets diffusion model (MSDM) to learn from each subset independently. This targeted learning approach reduces complexity and enhances the reconstruction of fine details. Furthermore, the integration of one-whole diffusion model (OWDM) with complete sinogram data acts as a global information constraint, which can reduce the possibility of generating erroneous or inconsistent sinogram information. Moreover, the OSMM's unsupervised learning framework provides strong robustness and generalizability, adapting seamlessly to varying sparsity levels of CT sinograms. This ensures consistent and reliable performance across different clinical scenarios. Experimental results demonstrate that OSMM outperforms traditional diffusion models in terms of image quality and noise resilience, offering a powerful and versatile solution for advanced CT imaging in sparse-view scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Ordered-subsets Multi-diffusion Model (OSMM) for sparse-view CT reconstruction.  OSMM tackles challenges associated with applying diffusion models directly to CT projection data, which is large and redundant. The key innovation is dividing the projection data into equal subsets and training multiple diffusion models (MSDM) on each subset. This reduces complexity and focuses learning on fine details. A separate diffusion model (OWDM) trained on the entire sinogram acts as a global constraint, preventing inconsistencies. OSMM is an unsupervised learning framework, offering robustness and generalizability to varying sparsity levels. Experimental results demonstrate superior image quality and noise resilience compared to traditional diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a relatively novel approach to sparse-view CT reconstruction that builds upon the recent success of diffusion models.  The central idea of dividing the projection data into ordered subsets and training multiple diffusion models is potentially significant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Clear Problem:**  The paper clearly identifies the limitations of directly applying diffusion models to large, redundant CT projection datasets. It highlights the difficulty in capturing fine details and the instability that can arise from the complexity of the data distribution.
    *   **Novelty of Approach:** The OSMM framework, particularly the combination of MSDM and OWDM, represents a novel strategy. MSDM facilitates the extraction of rich data features and clarity while providing ample texture, and OWDM improves image quality and resolution. It allows the model to learn both detailed subsets information and the whole sinogram by integrating multiple diffusion models from both MSDM and OWDM.
    *   **Unsupervised Learning:**  The unsupervised nature of OSMM is a major advantage, enabling adaptation to varying sparsity levels without requiring paired training data. This significantly enhances robustness and generalizability, particularly for different clinical scenarios.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The experimental results on the AAPM Challenge data, CIRS phantom, and preclinical mouse data show promising improvements in image quality and noise resilience compared to several established methods, using both quantitative metrics (PSNR, SSIM, MSE) and visual assessment.
    *   **Ablation Study**: the ablation study clearly illustrates that the two stages of MSDM and OWDM are both important for improving image reconstruction performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Complexity:** Training multiple diffusion models (MSDM) would inherently increase the computational complexity compared to single diffusion model approaches. The paper should delve deeper into the practical implications of this increased complexity and explore potential optimization strategies.

    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the experimental results are promising, the paper lacks a strong theoretical analysis to explain *why* the subset division and global constraint approach is effective. A more rigorous explanation would strengthen the argument.

    *   **Limited Comparison to Other Subset Methods:** The paper briefly mentions OSEM as inspiration but doesn't provide a direct comparison to other CT reconstruction methods that use ordered subsets. It is important to highlight the differences and advantages of the OSMM relative to this approach.

    *   **Justification of the Number of Subsets** It is necessary to provide a more precise rationale on how the number of subsets can affect overall image reconstruction.

**Significance:**

If the practical challenges associated with computational complexity can be addressed, OSMM has the potential to significantly advance sparse-view CT reconstruction. The unsupervised nature makes it adaptable to real-world clinical data, and the improved detail preservation and noise resilience could lead to reduced radiation doses and improved diagnostic accuracy.

The emphasis on clear theoretical exposition, along with a comprehensive understanding of its implementation complexities, and validation in realistic clinical scenarios, can greatly reinforce the overall contribution.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The OSMM represents a novel and promising approach with compelling experimental results, addressing a significant problem in the field. The unsupervised nature and the innovative combination of MSDM and OWDM are particularly notable strengths. However, the lack of detailed theoretical analysis, discussion of computational complexities, and the lack of rigorous comparison against subset reconstruction techniques detract from the novelty of the paper, meriting a lower score.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Air to Wear: Personalized 3D Digital Fashion with AR/VR Immersive 3D Sketching
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09998v1)
- **Authors**: Ying Zang, Yuanqi Hu, Xinyu Chen, Yuxia Xu, Suhui Wang, Chunan Yu, Lanyun Zhu, Deyi Ji, Xin Xu, Tianrun Chen
- **Abstract**: In the era of immersive consumer electronics, such as AR/VR headsets and smart devices, people increasingly seek ways to express their identity through virtual fashion. However, existing 3D garment design tools remain inaccessible to everyday users due to steep technical barriers and limited data. In this work, we introduce a 3D sketch-driven 3D garment generation framework that empowers ordinary users - even those without design experience - to create high-quality digital clothing through simple 3D sketches in AR/VR environments. By combining a conditional diffusion model, a sketch encoder trained in a shared latent space, and an adaptive curriculum learning strategy, our system interprets imprecise, free-hand input and produces realistic, personalized garments. To address the scarcity of training data, we also introduce KO3DClothes, a new dataset of paired 3D garments and user-created sketches. Extensive experiments and user studies confirm that our method significantly outperforms existing baselines in both fidelity and usability, demonstrating its promise for democratized fashion design on next-generation consumer platforms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method for creating personalized 3D digital fashion using AR/VR immersive sketching. It tackles the problem of accessibility in 3D garment design by allowing users to create garments simply by sketching in 3D environments within AR/VR. The core of the approach is a generative AI model that transforms these sketches into realistic, detailed garment models.  To address the limited availability of paired sketch-garment data, the authors present KO3DClothes, a new dataset of paired 3D garments and user-created sketches. The system combines a conditional diffusion model, a sketch encoder trained in a shared latent space, and an adaptive curriculum learning strategy.  The experiments and user studies indicate that the method outperforms existing baselines in both fidelity and usability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the integration of several existing techniques into a cohesive system specifically targeted at democratizing 3D fashion design within an AR/VR context. The use of AR/VR for 3D sketching directly addresses the usability challenge of traditional 3D modeling software. The multi-stage training regime is relatively novel. The dataset itself is another useful component to the research. This isn't a revolutionary breakthrough in any one individual component, but rather a well-engineered and applied solution.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is potentially high. Democratizing fashion design could have impacts beyond just personal expression. It could influence how garments are designed, manufactured, and consumed in the future, and promote greater customizability and reduce waste. Making 3D fashion design more accessible will bring non-professionals to the field. Also, as stated in the paper, such technology allows people to express themselves better in the immersive VR environment.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Usability Focus:** The paper prioritizes user accessibility, which is a major barrier in the field of 3D design.

    *   **Comprehensive System:** The integration of AR/VR input, a generative model, and a carefully designed training strategy creates a well-rounded system.

    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The KO3DClothes dataset helps overcome the scarcity of paired sketch-garment data.

    *   **Evaluations:** Thorough evaluations and user studies show that the system works well.
    *   **Multi-Stage Training Regime**: This is an efficient training method to solve the potential problem of limited data

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on AR/VR Hardware:** The solution is tied to the accessibility of AR/VR hardware, which, while growing, is still a limiting factor for some users.

    *   **Generative Model Limitations:**  Generative models, while powerful, may still struggle with creating truly unique and innovative designs beyond the training data distribution. The details of generated clothes remain simple as the focus of the research is shape accuracy.
    *   **Sketch complexity**: The quality of the sketch may still limit the final outcome.
    *   **Implicit Assumptions:** The paper operates under the implicit assumption that end users prefer to create novel virtual clothes designs instead of just buying existing templates.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach can influence future research in areas such as:

    *   Sketch-based modeling in AR/VR.
    *   Generative models for fashion design.
    *   Democratization of 3D content creation.

*   **Score Justification:** Given the incremental novelty and moderate potential impact, I assign a score of 7. This score reflects the work's solid engineering, good evaluations, potential for practical application, and dataset creation. However, there is a heavy dependence on existing technologies and hardware, and there are some limitations.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ServeGen: Workload Characterization and Generation of Large Language Model Serving in Production
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.09999v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxing Xiang, Xue Li, Kun Qian, Wenyuan Yu, Ennan Zhai, Xin Jin
- **Abstract**: With the widespread adoption of Large Language Models (LLMs), serving LLM inference requests has become an increasingly important task, attracting active research advancements. Practical workloads play an essential role in this process: they are critical for motivating and benchmarking serving techniques and systems. However, the existing understanding of real-world LLM serving workloads is limited due to the lack of a comprehensive workload characterization. Prior analyses remain insufficient in scale and scope, thus failing to fully capture intricate workload characteristics. In this paper, we fill the gap with an in-depth characterization of LLM serving workloads collected from our worldwide cloud inference serving service, covering not only language models but also emerging multimodal and reasoning models, and unveiling important new findings in each case. Moreover, based on our findings, we propose ServeGen, a principled framework for generating realistic LLM serving workloads by composing them on a per-client basis. A practical use case in production validates that ServeGen avoids 50% under-provisioning compared to naive workload generation, demonstrating ServeGen's advantage in performance benchmarking. We will open-source ServeGen to foster future research.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ServeGen: Workload Characterization and Generation of Large Language Model Serving in Production."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the lack of comprehensive and realistic workload characterization for Large Language Model (LLM) serving in production environments. The authors present an in-depth analysis of real-world LLM serving workloads collected from a large-scale cloud inference service. Their analysis covers language models, multimodal models, and reasoning models. They identify key characteristics, including bursty arrival patterns, shifting input/output length distributions, and client heterogeneity. Based on these findings, the authors propose ServeGen, a principled framework for generating realistic LLM serving workloads on a per-client basis. They demonstrate that ServeGen outperforms naive workload generation methods and provides a more accurate basis for performance benchmarking and system design. The authors offer insights into optimizing serving systems and aim to bridge the gap between research and production deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper makes several novel contributions:

*   **Comprehensive Characterization:**  The study offers a significantly more comprehensive and large-scale characterization of LLM serving workloads than previous work. It goes beyond basic metrics and delves into arrival patterns, input/output lengths, and client-level behavior across diverse model types (language, multimodal, reasoning). This level of detail is important for understanding and optimizing real-world LLM serving systems.
*   **Client Decomposition:** The client decomposition analysis is a key novelty.  It identifies the significant heterogeneity among clients and reveals how a small number of top clients drive much of the overall workload behavior. This insight has significant implications for workload modeling and system design, allowing for more targeted optimizations.  Previous work largely ignored client heterogeneity.
*   **ServeGen Framework:** The ServeGen framework is a valuable contribution.  It provides a practical tool for generating realistic LLM serving workloads based on the authors' empirical findings. The per-client modeling approach is a significant improvement over naive workload generation methods.
*   **Multimodal and Reasoning Workloads:** The inclusion of emerging multimodal and reasoning models in the characterization is novel.  These areas are rapidly developing, and a good understanding of their workload characteristics is essential for building efficient serving systems.
*   **Open-Source Contribution:** The promise to open-source ServeGen is very valuable, enabling other researchers to build on their work.

**Significance:**

*   **Bridging Research and Production:** The paper addresses a critical gap between LLM serving research and real-world deployment. By providing a realistic workload characterization and generation framework, the authors enable researchers to better evaluate their optimizations and design systems that are more likely to perform well in production.
*   **Informing System Design:** The paper's findings offer valuable insights for designing more efficient LLM serving systems.  For example, the bursty arrival patterns, shifting length distributions, and client heterogeneity suggest the need for adaptive scheduling, auto-scaling, and resource allocation techniques.
*   **Improving Benchmarking:** The paper highlights the limitations of naive workload generation methods and demonstrates that ServeGen can produce workloads that are more representative of real-world conditions. This is important for ensuring that benchmarks accurately reflect system performance.
*   **Understanding Emerging Workloads:** The characterization of multimodal and reasoning workloads provides a valuable starting point for optimizing serving systems for these emerging model types.

**Strengths:**

*   **Large-Scale Data:** The analysis is based on a significant dataset of real-world LLM serving workloads, which increases its credibility and generalizability.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The authors perform a thorough and insightful analysis, identifying several key characteristics that were not previously well understood.
*   **Practical Framework:** ServeGen provides a practical tool for generating realistic LLM serving workloads.
*   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is well-written and clearly organized, making it easy to understand the authors' contributions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Platform Specificity:** Although the dataset is large, it is sourced from a specific cloud inference service (Alibaba Bailian).  While many characteristics are likely general, some platform-specific nuances might affect the results. However, the authors have done a good job of sanitizing and abstracting the data to mitigate this limitation.
*   **Lack of Comparative Benchmarking:**  The evaluation primarily focuses on demonstrating the accuracy of ServeGen in replicating workload characteristics and shows the number of provisioned instances to meet SLOs. A more direct comparison of the performance of different LLM serving systems under ServeGen-generated workloads versus naive workloads would strengthen the evaluation. It would be very interesting to evaluate metrics like latency and throughput while using ServeGen generated work loads vs Naive.
*   **Limited Model Variety:** While the study covers a range of models, the specific models used are primarily from the Qwen family.  Including models from other families (e.g., OpenAI, Google) would increase the breadth of the analysis. But, because the analysis focuses on types of model and not specific model-optimizations, this is not a significant flaw.
*   **Plugin calls and prefix caching:** The authors themselves acknowledge that the framework is limited by aspects such plugin calls and prefix caching.

**Overall:**

This paper makes a significant contribution to the field of LLM serving by providing a comprehensive workload characterization and a practical framework for generating realistic workloads. The client decomposition analysis is particularly novel and valuable. While some limitations exist (such as platform specificity and the need for more extensive comparative benchmarking), the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.  The insights provided can substantially improve the design and evaluation of LLM serving systems, helping to bridge the gap between research and production deployment. The framework also allows for deeper investigation on other factors like multitenancy, security, and dynamic resource scaling.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 8/10

### SVA-ICL: Improving LLM-based Software Vulnerability Assessment via In-Context Learning and Information Fusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10008v1)
- **Authors**: Chaoyang Gao, Xiang Chen, Guangbei Zhang
- **Abstract**: Context: Software vulnerability assessment (SVA) is critical for identifying, evaluating, and prioritizing security weaknesses in software applications. Objective: Despite the increasing application of large language models (LLMs) in various software engineering tasks, their effectiveness in SVA remains underexplored. Method: To address this gap, we introduce a novel approach SVA-ICL, which leverages in-context learning (ICL) to enhance LLM performance. Our approach involves the selection of high-quality demonstrations for ICL through information fusion, incorporating both source code and vulnerability descriptions. For source code, we consider semantic, lexical, and syntactic similarities, while for vulnerability descriptions, we focus on textual similarity. Based on the selected demonstrations, we construct context prompts and consider DeepSeek-V2 as the LLM for SVA-ICL. Results: We evaluate the effectiveness of SVA-ICL using a large-scale dataset comprising 12,071 C/C++ vulnerabilities. Experimental results demonstrate that SVA-ICL outperforms state-of-the-art SVA baselines in terms of Accuracy, F1-score, and MCC measures. Furthermore, ablation studies highlight the significance of component customization in SVA-ICL, such as the number of demonstrations, the demonstration ordering strategy, and the optimal fusion ratio of different modalities. Conclusion: Our findings suggest that leveraging ICL with information fusion can effectively improve the effectiveness of LLM-based SVA, warranting further research in this direction.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper introduces SVA-ICL, a novel approach to software vulnerability assessment (SVA) leveraging in-context learning (ICL) with large language models (LLMs). SVA-ICL enhances LLM performance by selecting high-quality demonstrations through information fusion, considering both source code (semantic, lexical, syntactic similarities) and vulnerability descriptions (textual similarity). The approach is evaluated on a large C/C++ vulnerability dataset, demonstrating improvements over existing SVA baselines. The paper also explores the impact of various factors like the number of demonstrations, ordering strategies, and modality fusion ratios through ablation studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in applying ICL with LLMs to the SVA task, which the authors claim has not been thoroughly investigated. The information fusion approach, combining source code and vulnerability description analysis, is another noteworthy contribution. The use of syntactic features from source code via tree-sitter is also innovative. However, the core ICL methodology itself isn't new, it's the tailored application to SVA and the associated customization that provides the novelty.
*   **Significance:** The potential significance of this research is in automating and improving the accuracy of SVA, which is critical for software security. Demonstrating that LLMs can effectively perform this task with appropriate prompting and information fusion is a valuable step. The large-scale dataset created with CVSS v3 scores is another useful contribution that can aid future research efforts. The ablation studies offer insights into how to best configure the ICL process for SVA, guiding future work.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the importance and challenges of SVA.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** SVA-ICL is presented in a structured and understandable manner.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental setup is rigorous, including comparisons with relevant baselines and thorough ablation studies.
    *   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The creation and use of a large, up-to-date vulnerability dataset contributes to the field.
    *   **Insightful Ablation Studies:** The exploration of parameter settings and their impact on performance provides practical guidance for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The core techniques (ICL, LLMs) are not fundamentally new. The novelty is in the specific application and adaptation to SVA. It's still an incremental improvement based on well established methods.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Code Representation:** While the paper uses several features from source code, a deeper exploration of more advanced code representation techniques could enhance the approach further.
    *   **Dependence on LLM Performance:** The approach is inherently limited by the capabilities of the underlying LLM. While the paper justifies the use of DeepSeek-V2, future LLMs may render the customization obsolete.
    *   **Limited Explanation of the "Why":** While the ablation studies show what works, the 'why' behind some results is speculative. More analysis connecting specific ICL demonstration choices to SVA performance at a granular level would strengthen the understanding.
    *   **Generalizability to Other Languages:** The evaluation focuses solely on C/C++. It needs to be shown whether these findings are applicable in other languages (java, python etc).

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence future research in SVA by highlighting the potential of LLMs and providing a roadmap for effective ICL-based approaches. The dataset and ablation study results offer a valuable resource for the community. Also helps in creating an automatic system for finding and fixing vulnerabilities.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the above analysis, while the paper presents a well-executed and valuable contribution, the incremental nature of the core techniques limits its overall impact. The innovation is primarily in the application and customization of existing methods to the SVA task. The LLM approach also needs to be shown generalizability for different languages.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### ImagineBench: Evaluating Reinforcement Learning with Large Language Model Rollouts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10010v1)
- **Authors**: Jing-Cheng Pang, Kaiyuan Li, Yidi Wang, Si-Hang Yang, Shengyi Jiang, Yang Yu
- **Abstract**: A central challenge in reinforcement learning (RL) is its dependence on extensive real-world interaction data to learn task-specific policies. While recent work demonstrates that large language models (LLMs) can mitigate this limitation by generating synthetic experience (noted as imaginary rollouts) for mastering novel tasks, progress in this emerging field is hindered due to the lack of a standard benchmark. To bridge this gap, we introduce ImagineBench, the first comprehensive benchmark for evaluating offline RL algorithms that leverage both real rollouts and LLM-imaginary rollouts. The key features of ImagineBench include: (1) datasets comprising environment-collected and LLM-imaginary rollouts; (2) diverse domains of environments covering locomotion, robotic manipulation, and navigation tasks; and (3) natural language task instructions with varying complexity levels to facilitate language-conditioned policy learning. Through systematic evaluation of state-of-the-art offline RL algorithms, we observe that simply applying existing offline RL algorithms leads to suboptimal performance on unseen tasks, achieving 35.44% success rate in hard tasks in contrast to 64.37% of method training on real rollouts for hard tasks. This result highlights the need for algorithm advancements to better leverage LLM-imaginary rollouts. Additionally, we identify key opportunities for future research: including better utilization of imaginary rollouts, fast online adaptation and continual learning, and extension to multi-modal tasks. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/LAMDA-RL/ImagineBench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "ImagineBench: Evaluating Reinforcement Learning with Large Language Model Rollouts" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ImagineBench, a new benchmark designed to evaluate offline reinforcement learning (RL) algorithms that leverage both real-world experience and synthetic experience generated by large language models (LLMs), referred to as "imaginary rollouts."  The benchmark consists of datasets combining environment-collected and LLM-generated rollouts, covers diverse environments (locomotion, robotic manipulation, navigation), and includes natural language task instructions with varying complexity. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art offline RL algorithms on ImagineBench and find that simply applying existing methods often leads to suboptimal performance compared to training solely on real data.  They identify key areas for future research, including better utilization of imaginary rollouts, fast online adaptation, and extension to multi-modal tasks. The code and datasets are publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of ImagineBench lies primarily in its comprehensive approach to benchmarking RL with LLM-generated synthetic data. While the individual components (using LLMs for synthetic data, offline RL, standard RL environments) are not entirely new, the combination and standardization are valuable. Previous work in RL with LLMs often used custom environments and evaluation protocols, making it difficult to compare results reliably. ImagineBench addresses this by providing a common ground. However, the *algorithmic contributions are limited*, as the paper focuses on *evaluating existing algorithms* rather than proposing novel techniques for leveraging LLM rollouts.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the increasing interest in using LLMs to enhance RL, particularly in settings where real-world interaction data is scarce or expensive to collect. ImagineBench provides a crucial tool for researchers to systematically assess and compare different approaches in this emerging field. The paper's findings highlight a clear gap: existing offline RL algorithms don't effectively utilize LLM-generated data. This result identifies a significant challenge and motivates the development of new algorithms specifically designed for this hybrid setting.
    The public release of ImagineBench is a major strength. Standardized benchmarks drive progress by facilitating comparisons, reproducibility, and the identification of bottlenecks. By open-sourcing their code and data, the authors are encouraging further research and development in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive Benchmark: ImagineBench offers a diverse set of environments, tasks, and difficulty levels.
    *   Standardized Evaluation:  It provides a consistent framework for evaluating RL algorithms with LLM rollouts, which enables more reliable comparisons.
    *   Open Source:  The public release of the code and datasets is a major contribution to the community.
    *   Clear Identification of Challenges: The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods and identifies promising directions for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited Algorithmic Contributions: The paper primarily focuses on benchmarking and doesn't introduce novel RL algorithms for leveraging LLM rollouts.
    *   LLM Rollout Quality: While the paper acknowledges the varying quality of LLM rollouts, further analysis and methods for filtering or improving them would have been valuable.
    *   Environment Dependence: The specific findings about algorithm performance might be somewhat dependent on the choice of environments and LLM used in ImagineBench.
*   **Potential Impact:** ImagineBench has the potential to significantly accelerate research in RL with LLMs. It provides a standardized platform for developing and evaluating new algorithms, which could lead to more efficient and effective ways of leveraging LLM knowledge for embodied agents. The benchmark could also inspire the development of new techniques for improving the quality and diversity of LLM-generated rollouts.
**Overall:**
The creation of ImagineBench is a timely and valuable contribution to the field of reinforcement learning, particularly in light of the increasing interest in utilizing LLMs to improve sample efficiency and generalization. It provides a much-needed standardized platform for evaluating algorithms that integrate real-world data and LLM-generated data, offering valuable insights into the current limitations of existing methods and pointing towards promising research directions. While the paper lacks novel algorithmic contributions, the comprehensive nature of the benchmark and the open release of the code and datasets make it a significant resource for the RL community.
